Mode contract

recorded (outcome.json contract field)

Verbatim, as recorded with the run and sent to the agents.

- primitive to instantiate: `mult_add_int_18x19` (precision INT19)
- computes: out = ax*ay + bx + chainin
- input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19]
- output ports: result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19]
- arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
- registers: on `clk`, the block registers its operands (`ax`, `ay`, `bx`) at one clock edge and `result` at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in `result` after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. `chainin` is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches `result` 2 cycles after it is applied. `chainout` is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value `result` loads at the next clock edge unless that edge clears it, one cycle before `result` shows it.
- reset: `reset` is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
- power-up: the block's registers have no initial value. Each loads its input at every clock edge where `reset` is low, so, for a block whose inputs, `chainin` included, are defined, the result is defined from the second edge after power-up, with or without a reset. A rewrite may not rely on the result before then.
- tie-off: `chainin` = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's `chainout` or a constant may drive it); `scanin` = 0 always (the operand scan chain is not used: `ay` is driven directly, and `scanout` is left unconnected); `mode_sigs` = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
- connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. `chainout` reaches only the next block's `chainin`, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.

prompt
Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mult_add_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19]
  • output ports: result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge unless that edge clears it, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the block's registers have no initial value. Each loads its input at every clock edge where reset is low, so, for a block whose inputs, chainin included, are defined, the result is defined from the second edge after power-up, with or without a reset. A rewrite may not rely on the result before then.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); scanin = 0 always (the operand scan chain is not used: ay is driven directly, and scanout is left unconnected); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of result (clock-to-Q)0.060
chainout: input register clock-to-Q + delay, to the output pin0.060 + 2.140 = 2.200
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
Candidate regions

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Goal

Propose a correct rewrite onto the block. The loop measures it against the original design on area × delay; you are not asked to predict the result.

Output
Tiers
  • T1: The block replaces the region's arithmetic as it stands: the same operations on the same operands, now inside the block.
  • T2: The region's arithmetic is regrouped to fit the block (products regrouped into the block's sums, registers moved), computing the same values.
  • NONE: You propose no rewrite this time. The plan states why, from what you were given: no correct rewrite, or nothing left to try. The run then ends and keeps the best result so far if its median over every seed is below the original's, and the original design otherwise (also when no rewrite has beaten it).
Header

Write exactly this header, each key at the start of its own line, then the plan:

TIER: <exactly one of T1, T2, NONE>
REGIONS: <the converted candidate ids, comma-separated; NONE when TIER is NONE>
SHARED_REDUCTION: <the signal or module the converted products are summed in; NONE for a single region>
BLOCKS: <how many blocks the rewrite instantiates, one integer>
MAPPING: <which design signals go to which block ports>
PLAN: <the plan, stating everything listed below>

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left: a correctness reason, or "not chosen this pass"; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the TIER line; read the source first.
  • Your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.
Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_tpu_os_top.v) and the block's behavioural model (mult_add_int_18x19.v) are in sim_reference/.

SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE (no block is chained; each block holds one product and, in activation, that product's own addend. The 4 norm products marked sum_in=top are summed in pool's window-4 adder at line 5392, downstream of norm's out_data port — that sum stays in soft logic.)
BLOCKS: 32
MAPPING:

  • 16 blocks in module activation (u_act_mul0 .. u_act_mul15), one per lane i = 0..15:

    • clk = clk
    • reset = act_blk_rst = reset | ~enable_activation | ~(in_data_available | activation_in_progress) | ~activation_type
    • mode_sigs = 12'b0, scanin = 19'b0, chainin = 64'b0
    • ax = {10'b0, inp_data_flopped[i*8 +: 8]} (18 bits)
    • ay = {11'b0, data_slope_flopped[i*8 +: 8]} (19 bits)
    • bx = {28'b0, data_intercept_flopped[i*8 +: 8]} (36 bits)
    • result[7:0] -> intercept_applied_data_internal[i*8 +: 8]; chainout and scanout left unconnected

  • 16 blocks in module norm (u_norm_mul0 .. u_norm_mul15), one per lane i = 0..15:

    • clk = clk
    • reset = norm_blk_rst = reset | ~enable_norm | ~(in_data_available | norm_in_progress)
    • mode_sigs = 12'b0, scanin = 19'b0, chainin = 64'b0, bx = 36'b0
    • ax = {10'b0, norm_ax_i} where wire [7:0] norm_ax_i = validity_mask[i] ? (inp_data[i*8 +: 8] - mean) : inp_data[i*8 +: 8];
    • ay = {11'b0, norm_ay_i} where wire [7:0] norm_ay_i = validity_mask[i] ? inv_var : 8'd1;
    • result[7:0] -> variance_applied_data[i*8 +: 8]; chainout and scanout left unconnected

PLAN:

ASSUMES: activation_type does not change while the activation datapath is active, i.e. while in_data_available | activation_in_progress is high (line 5477). It is a cfg register written only over APB (line 4913) with reset value 1 (line 4838); the software configures it before writing start_tpu (line 4885) and the control FSM only raises the activation phase after start (lines 5259-5323). Under this assumption the hold cases of lines 5482-5485 (active, activation_type==0) only ever hold the value 0, because every edge on which the block is not active writes 0 to those registers (lines 5467-5471 and 5522-5526); driving the hard block's synchronous reset instead therefore produces the same value.

ASSUMES: inv_var and validity_mask (= validity_mask_a_rows, line 5902) hold the same value on two consecutive clock edges while the norm datapath is active (line 5059). They are cfg registers written only over APB (lines 4889 and 4894, reset values at lines 4823 and 4829), and the software writes them before start_tpu (line 4885); the norm phase is entered only after start (lines 5259-5306). The rewrite samples them one edge earlier than lines 5068-5070 do, so it matches whenever they are steady across the pair of edges.

Both assumptions hold on the reference testbench's stimulus as well: the drive file configures 0x08/0x0a/0x3a/0x00 before each apb_write(8'h04, 32'h1) and only reads 0x04 while a run is in flight, and in the random phase address 0x04 is never written, so start_tpu stays 0, start_mat_mul stays 0, c_data_available is held at 0 (line 2305-2307) and both norm and activation sit in their zeroing branches regardless of the random writes to 0x0a/0x20/0x3a.

Why these regions, and why the others are left
  • qmult.$mul$...4728$349(sum_in=seq_mac), 256 instances — LEFT, capacity. One block of this mode occupies a whole DSP tile and the device has 132 of them; this region needs 256 blocks, so it cannot be placed. (A partial conversion is not offered: the region is one shape repeated 256 times and converting a subset would still need more than 132 tiles once combined with anything else, and converting a strict subset changes no arithmetic the region does not already do.) Note also that its sum is a 1-cycle accumulator loop (out_temp <= out_temp + mul_out_temp_reg, lines 4697-4704): the block's result is 2 cycles behind its operands and chainout may not reach general routing, so bx <= result feedback would give a 2-cycle accumulation loop and could not reproduce it anyway.
  • activation $470, $474, $478, $482, $486, $490, $494, $498, $502, $506, $510, $514, $518, $522, $526, $530 — CONVERTED. Each is one lane of lines 5482/5485: an 8x8 product truncated to 8 bits, added one stage later to data_intercept_delayed. Each becomes one block computing ax*ay + bx.
  • norm $368, $371, $374, $377, $380, $383, $386, $389, $392, $395, $398, $401, $404, $407, $410, $413 — CONVERTED. Each is one lane of line 5070 (and the mask-off pass-through of line 5074): one 8x8 product truncated to 8 bits. Each becomes one block computing ax*ay with bx = 0. The downstream adder chain in pool (line 5392) that the four sum_in=top ids refer to is not moved into any block: norm.out_data is a module port read by pool and by top, so it must stay visible.
Blocks added

32 blocks, 32 DSP tiles of the 132 available. No block is chained: every chainin is tied to 64'b0 and every chainout is left unconnected. Every scanin is 19'b0, every mode_sigs is 12'b0, scanout is unconnected. Do not copy the mult_add_int_18x19 model into the design file — instantiate it only.

Register stages, before and after
  • activation, each of the 16 regions. Before: 2 register stages from the block's operands to the sum — data_slope_flopped/inp_data_flopped/data_intercept_flopped (stage 0, the operands) -> slope_applied_data_internal and data_intercept_delayed (stage 1) -> intercept_applied_data_internal (stage 2). After: 2 stages, the block's own — operand registers ax_reg/ay_reg/bx_reg (stage 1) -> result (stage 2). The multiply is retimed one stage later, across slope_applied_data_internal: the product of two registered values is unchanged by registering the operands and multiplying a cycle later. Counting from the module input, inp_data -> out_data_internal is 3 stages before and 3 after.
  • norm, each of the 16 regions. Before: 2 register stages from inp_data to variance_applied_data — mean_applied_data (stage 1) -> variance_applied_data (stage 2). After: 2 stages — the block's ax_reg (stage 1, which is mean_applied_data) -> result (stage 2). The - mean / mask select stays in soft logic ahead of ax, exactly where line 5069/5073 put it.
Edits in module norm (lines 5007-5109)

  1. Delete the reg declarations of mean_applied_data and variance_applied_data (lines 5023-5024). Declare instead:

    • wire [63:0] norm_res0 .. norm_res15;
    • wire [DESIGN_SIZE*DWIDTH-1:0] variance_applied_data = {norm_res15[7:0], norm_res14[7:0], ..., norm_res1[7:0], norm_res0[7:0]}; (lane 15 at the top, lane 0 at the bottom, so that bits [i*8 +: 8] are lane i, as in the original).
    • wire norm_blk_rst = reset | ~enable_norm | ~(in_data_available | norm_in_progress);
    • 16 pairs of 8-bit wires, written out explicitly so the subtraction truncates to 8 bits exactly as line 5069 does:
      wire [7:0] norm_ax0 = validity_mask[0] ? (inp_data[0*8 +: 8] - mean) : inp_data[0*8 +: 8];
      wire [7:0] norm_ay0 = validity_mask[0] ? inv_var : 8'd1;
      ... through index 15. Do not inline the subtraction into the .ax(...) port expression: in an 18-bit context it would not wrap at 8 bits.
  2. In the always block: delete mean_applied_data <= 0; and variance_applied_data <= 0; at lines 5051-5052 and at 5098-5099, and delete the whole for loop of lines 5067-5076 (its entire body was those two registers). Leave cycle_count, out_data_available_internal, done_norm_internal, norm_in_progress, in_data_available_flopped and inp_data_flopped exactly as they are. reg [31:0] i; may stay declared and unused.
  3. Leave assign out_data_internal = variance_applied_data; (line 5107) as it is — it now reads the wire.
  4. Add the 16 instances, pattern (index 0 shown, repeat for 1..15):
    ```
    mult_add_int_18x19 u_norm_mul0 (
    .clk(clk), .reset(norm_blk_rst), .mode_sigs(12'b0),
    .ax({10'b0, norm_ax0}), .ay({11'b0, norm_ay0}),
    .bx(36'b0), .chainin(64'b0), .scanin(19'b0),
    .result(norm_res0), .chainout(), .scanout());
    ```
Edits in module activation (lines 5417-5626)

    • wire [63:0] act_res0 .. act_res15;
    • wire [DESIGN_SIZE*DWIDTH-1:0] intercept_applied_data_internal = {act_res15[7:0], act_res14[7:0], ..., act_res1[7:0], act_res0[7:0]};
    • wire act_blk_rst = reset | ~enable_activation | ~(in_data_available | activation_in_progress) | ~activation_type;
  3. Keep the zeroing of data_intercept_flopped (5471, 5526) and of relu_applied_data_internal.
  4. In the for loop at 5480-5489: in the activation_type==1'b1 branch keep only line 5483 (data_intercept_flopped[i*8 +: 8] <= data_intercept[i*8 +: 8];) and delete lines 5482, 5484, 5485. Leave the else (ReLU) branch at 5487 untouched. Leave the cycle_count / out_data_available_internal / done_activation_internal / activation_in_progress logic at 5492-5519 untouched.
  5. Leave line 5534 (assign out_data_internal = (activation_type) ? intercept_applied_data_internal : relu_applied_data_internal;) unchanged — it now reads the wire. Leave the two LUT always blocks (5540-5577) and the address decoder (5580-5619) unchanged.
  6. Add the 16 instances, pattern (index 0 shown, repeat for 1..15):
    ```
    mult_add_int_18x19 u_act_mul0 (
    .clk(clk), .reset(act_blk_rst), .mode_sigs(12'b0),
    .ax({10'b0, inp_data_flopped[0*8 +: 8]}),
    .ay({11'b0, data_slope_flopped[0*8 +: 8]}),
    .bx({28'b0, data_intercept_flopped[0*8 +: 8]}),
    .chainin(64'b0), .scanin(19'b0),
    .result(act_res0), .chainout(), .scanout());
    ```
Why this is the same behavior

Write E for the block's reset expression and note that in both modules E is exactly the negation of the "update" branch condition of the original always block (plus ~activation_type in activation, covered by the first ASSUMES). For a register that the original writes with 0 on every edge where E is high, the block's synchronous clear reproduces it.

  • norm: the block's ax_reg after edge k is E(k) ? 0 : (mask ? inp_data-mean : inp_data) sampled just before edge k — bit for bit the original mean_applied_data (lines 5051/5069/5073/5098). result after edge k is E(k) ? 0 : ax_reg(k-1)*ay_reg(k-1), and ay_reg(k-1) is inv_var for a masked-in lane and 1 for a masked-out lane, so the product is mean_applied_data(k-1)*inv_var or mean_applied_data(k-1) — lines 5070/5074, with result[7:0] giving the same 8-bit truncation. bx and chainin are 0, so the 64-bit wrap is never reached. mean_applied_data has no other reader (only lines 5070 and 5074), so moving it inside the block hides nothing that is read elsewhere.
  • activation: ax_reg/ay_reg after edge k-1 hold inp_data_flopped/data_slope_flopped as of edge k-2, so the product formed for result(k) is exactly the product line 5482 forms for slope_applied_data_internal(k-1); bx_reg(k-1) holds data_intercept_flopped(k-2), which is exactly what data_intercept_delayed(k-1) holds (line 5484). Hence result(k)[7:0] = (product + intercept) mod 256, equal to the original (product mod 256 + intercept) mod 256 of lines 5482+5485. slope_applied_data_internal and data_intercept_delayed have no other readers (only line 5485), so they may live inside the block; inp_data_flopped does have another reader (line 5461) and is kept outside and fed into ax as well.
  • Power-up: the blocks' registers are X until the first clock edge that samples reset/E low... both E expressions include the top-level reset, which the testbench holds high for its first 4 cycles, so every block register is cleared at the first edge and result is defined from the first edge onward — well before the first compared cycle. No added soft register relies on an initial value: the only new state is inside the blocks.
  • Mid-run resets: E is re-derived combinationally from reset every cycle, so a reset pulse at any time clears the block exactly on the edge at which lines 5050/5466 clear the originals.

DESIGN_CHANGE: The 256 seq_mac MACs are where almost all of this design's multiplies are, and they are unreachable for two independent reasons — 256 blocks against 132 DSP tiles, and the 1-cycle accumulator loop at lines 4697-4704 against the block's 2-cycle operand-to-result latency with no general-routing path out of chainout. Restructuring seq_mac so the accumulation is a 2-cycle (even/odd interleaved) loop, and time-multiplexing the 16x16 array down to at most 132 physical MACs, would let that region use this block and would dominate any gain available from norm and activation.

1	// mult_add_int_18x19: result = ax*ay + bx + chainin, unsigned, the product and bx zero-extended
2	// to 64 bits, modulo 2^64 (the sum wraps at the result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19] in;
4	// result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19] out. mode_sigs and scanin are not read (the scan chain is
5	// unused and tied to 0 in every spec).
6	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
7	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx and chainin alike); the product and
8	// the sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
9	// Cycles: ax, ay, bx and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
10	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (1 cycle from the
11	// inputs).
12	// scanout is the ay register (ay to scanout 1), as before; nothing specifies it.
13	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
14	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
15	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
16	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
17	// X until the first clear.
18	module mult_add_int_18x19 (
19	    input clk,
20	    input reset,
21	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
22	    input [17:0] ax,
23	    input [18:0] ay,
24	    input [35:0] bx,
25	    input [63:0] chainin,
26	    input [18:0] scanin,
27	    output reg [63:0] result,
28	    output [63:0] chainout,
29	    output [18:0] scanout
30	);
31	
32	    // stage 1: the input registers
33	    reg [17:0] ax_reg;
34	    reg [18:0] ay_reg;
35	    reg [35:0] bx_reg;
36	    reg [63:0] chainin_reg;
37	
38	    // combinational middle: the product, the addend and chainin, from the registers
39	    wire [36:0] prod = ax_reg * ay_reg;                          // 18x19 -> 37, exact
40	    wire [63:0] sum  = prod + bx_reg + chainin_reg;              // zero-extended, modulo 2^64
41	
42	    always @(posedge clk) begin
43	        if (reset) begin
44	            result <= 0;
45	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
46	        end
47	        else begin
48	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; chainin_reg <= chainin;
49	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
50	        end
51	    end
52	
53	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
54	    assign scanout  = ay_reg;
55	
56	endmodule

5033	assign done_norm = (enable_norm) ? done_norm_internal : 1'b1;
5034

5036	//on each clock edge, if in_data_available is 1, then we will normalize the inputs.
5037	
5038	//the code uses the funky part-select syntax. example:
5039	//wire [7:0] byteN = word[byte_num*8 +: 8];
5040	//byte_num*8 is the starting point. 8 is the width is the part-select (has to be constant).in_data_available
5041	//+: indicates the part-select increases from the starting point
5042	//-: indicates the part-select decreases from the starting point
5043	//another example:
5044	//loc = 3;

5060	        cycle_count <= cycle_count + 1;
5061	        //Let's apply mean and variance as the input data comes in.
5062	        //We have a pipeline here. First stage does the add (to apply the mean)
5063	        //and second stage does the multiplication (to apply the variance).
5064	        //Note: the following loop is not a loop across multiple columns of data.
5065	        //This loop will run in 2 cycle on the same column of data that comes into

5071	            end 
5072	            else begin
5073	                mean_applied_data[i*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH] <= (inp_data[i*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH]);
5074	                variance_applied_data[i*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH] <= (mean_applied_data[i*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH]);
5075	            end
5076	        end
5077	
5078	        //Out data is available starting with the second clock cycle because 
5079	        //in the first cycle, we only apply the mean.

5081	            out_data_available_internal <= 1;
5082	        end

5084	        //When we've normalized values N times, where N is the matmul

5087	        //column of data). We can call thi

5417	module activation(
5418	    input activation_type,
5419	    input enable_activation,
5420	    input in_data_available,
5421	    input [`DESIGN_SIZE*`DWIDTH-1:0] inp_data,
5422	    output [`DESIGN_SIZE*`DWIDTH-1:0] out_data,
5423	    output out_data_available,
5424	    input [`MASK_WIDTH-1:0] validity_mask,
5425	    output done_activation,
5426	    input clk,
5427	    input reset
5428	);
5429	
5430	reg  done_activation_internal;
5431	reg  out_data_available_internal;
5432	wire [`DESIGN_SIZE*`DWIDTH-1:0] out_data_internal;
5433	reg [`DESIGN_SIZE*`DWIDTH-1:0] slope_applied_data_internal;
5434	reg [`DESIGN_SIZE*`DWIDTH-1:0] intercept_applied_data_internal;
5435	reg [`DESIGN_SIZE*`DWIDTH-1:0] relu_applied_data_internal;
5436	reg [31:0] i;
5437	reg [31:0] cycle_count;
5438	reg activation_in_progress;
5439	
5440	reg [(`DESIGN_SIZE*4)-1:0] address;
5441	reg [(`DESIGN_SIZE*8)-1:0] data_slope;
5442	reg [(`DESIGN_SIZE*8)-1:0] data_slope_flopped;
5443	reg [(`DESIGN_SIZE*8)-1:0] data_intercept;
5444	reg [(`DESIGN_SIZE*8)-1:0] data_intercept_delayed;
5445	reg [(`DESIGN_SIZE*8)-1:0] data_intercept_flopped;
5446	
5447	reg in_data_available_flopped;
5448	reg [`DESIGN_SIZE*`DWIDTH-1:0] inp_data_flopped;
5449	
5450	always @(posedge clk) begin
5451	  if (reset) begin
5452	    inp_data_flopped <= 0;
5453	    data_slope_flopped <= 0;
5454	  end else begin
5455	    inp_data_flopped <= inp_data;
5456	    data_slope_flopped <= data_slope;
5457	  end
5458	end
5459	
5460	// If the activation block is not enabled, just forward the input data
5461	assign out_data             = enable_activation ? out_data_internal : inp_data_flopped;
5462	assign done_activation      = enable_activation ? done_activation_internal : 1'b1;
5463	assign out_data_available   = enable_activation ? out_data_available_internal : in_data_available_flopped;
5464	
5465	always @(posedge clk) begin
5466	   if (reset || ~enable_activation) begin
5467	      slope_applied_data_internal     <= 0;
5468	      intercept_applied_data_internal <= 0; 
5469	      relu_applied_data_internal      <= 0; 
5470	      data_intercept_delayed      <= 0;
5471	      data_intercept_flopped      <= 0;
5472	      done_activation_internal    <= 0;
5473	      out_data_available_internal <= 0;
5474	      cycle_count                 <= 0;
5475	      activation_in_progress      <= 0;
5476	      in_data_available_flopped <= in_data_available;
5477	   end else if(in_data_available || activation_in_progress) begin
5478	      cycle_count <= cycle_count + 1;
5479	
5480	      for (i = 0; i < `DESIGN_SIZE; i=i+1) begin
5481	         if(activation_type==1'b1) begin // tanH
5482	            slope_applied_data_internal[i*`DWIDTH +:`DWIDTH] <= data_slope_flopped[i*8 +: 8] * inp_data_flopped[i*`DWIDTH +:`DWIDTH];
5483	            data_intercept_flopped[i*8 +: 8] <= data_intercept[i*8 +: 8];
5484	            data_intercept_delayed[i*8 +: 8] <= data_intercept_flopped[i*8 +: 8];
5485	            intercept_applied_data_internal[i*`DWIDTH +:`DWIDTH] <= slope_applied_data_internal[i*`DWIDTH +:`DWIDTH] + data_intercept_delayed[i*8 +: 8];
5486	         end else begin // ReLU
5487	            relu_applied_data_internal[i*`DWIDTH +:`DWIDTH] <= inp_data[i*`DWIDTH] ? {`DWIDTH{1'b0}} : inp_data[i*`DWIDTH +:`DWIDTH];
5488	         end
5489	      end   
5490	
5491	      //TANH needs 1 extra cycle
5492	      if (activation_type==1'b1) begin
5493	         if (cycle_count==3) begin
5494	            out_data_available_internal <= 1;
5495	         end
5496	      end else begin
5497	         if (cycle_count==2) begin
5498	           out_data_available_internal <= 1;
5499	         end
5500	      end
5501	
5502	      //TANH needs 1 extra cycle
5503	      if (activation_type==1'b1) begin
5504	        if(cycle_count==(`DESIGN_SIZE+2)) begin
5505	           done_activation_internal <= 1'b1;
5506	           activation_in_progress <= 0;
5507	        end

1	        // tpu_like.small.os.fixed at its top: the ws drive sequence on the os register map and its stride-16
2	        // layout. Run 1 matmul alone; the APB write that pulses pe_reset (the design's own one-cycle pulse,
3	        // with the array quiet: the protocol); a full reset; run 2 every block; a full reset; run 3 matmul
4	        // and norm; the random phase (APB traffic on the register set except the start/done word, BRAM
5	        // writes, reset 1 in 1024). Under START_ONLY the full resets and the random resets are left out.
6	        // Under PE_RESET_IN_FLIGHT (the diagnostic) one more APB write of the pe_reset bit lands while run
7	        // 2's multiply is in flight, which the protocol excludes.
8	        fill_matrices;
9	        phase = "run1";
10	        load_matrices(0, 10'd0, 10'd0);
11	        apb_write(8'h0e, 32'd0); apb_write(8'h12, 32'd0); apb_write(8'h16, 32'd768);
12	        apb_write(8'h00, 32'h1); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h1);
13	        wait_done(400); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h0);
14	        phase = "readback1"; read_c(10'd768, "run 1 (matmul)");
15	        phase = "pe_reset"; apb_write(8'h04, 32'h8000);   // the pe_reset bit, array quiet
16	        zero_cycles(4);
17	        if (`MIDRUN_RESETS) begin phase = "reset-pulse"; full_reset; end
18	        phase = "run2";
19	        load_matrices(1, 10'd256, 10'd256);
20	        apb_write(8'h0e, 32'd256); apb_write(8'h12, 32'd256); apb_write(8'h16, 32'd768);
21	        config_blocks(32'hf); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h1);
22	`ifdef PE_RESET_IN_FLIGHT
23	        zero_cycles(30); phase = "pe_reset-live"; apb_write(8'h04, 32'h8000);   // a one-cycle pe_reset with products in flight
24	`endif
25	        wait_done(400); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h0);
26	        phase = "readback2"; read_c(10'd768, "run 2 (every block)");
27	        if (`MIDRUN_RESETS) begin phase = "reset-pulse"; full_reset; end
28	        phase = "run3";
29	        load_matrices(2, 10'd512, 10'd512);
30	        apb_write(8'h0e, 32'd512); apb_write(8'h12, 32'd512); apb_write(8'h16, 32'd768);
31	        config_blocks(32'h3); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h1);
32	        wait_done(400); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h0);
33	        phase = "readback3"; read_c(10'd768, "run 3 (matmul and norm, after reset)");
34	        phase = "random"; ph = 0;
35	        for (i = 0; i < `RANDOM_CYCLES; i = i + 1) begin
36	            tick; set_data;
37	            case (ph)
38	                0: begin PSEL = 1; draw; PWRITE = r32[0]; PADDR = reg_addr(r32[5:1]); if (PADDR == 8'h04) PADDR = 8'h00; PENABLE = 0; apb_addr = PADDR; apb_write_q = PWRITE; apb_wdata = PWDATA; end   // no random start or pe_reset write: the protocol
39	                1: begin PSEL = 1; PENABLE = 1; PADDR = apb_addr; PWRITE = apb_write_q; PWDATA = apb_wdata; end   // set_data redrew them; the access cycle keeps the setup cycle's (APB: stable through setup and access)
40	                2: begin PSEL = 0; PENABLE = 0; PWRITE = 0; end
41	                default: begin PSEL = 0; PENABLE = 0; end
42	            endcase
43	            ph = (ph + 1) % 4;
44	            draw; bram_we_a_ext = (r32[1:0] == 0) ? 16'hffff : 16'h0; bram_we_b_ext = (r32[3:2] == 0) ? 16'hffff : 16'h0;
45	            draw; reset = (`MIDRUN_RESETS && (r32 % 1024) == 0); resetn = ~reset;
46	        end
47	        PSEL = 0; PENABLE = 0; PWRITE = 0; bram_we_a_ext = 0; bram_we_b_ext = 0;

1	    // tpu_like.small.ws top (from the hand-written testbench of 2026-10-19): the design driven as its
2	    // software drives it. Matrices written into the two BRAMs through the external ports (word k of A at
3	    // address_mat_a + k, byte i = A[i][k]; word k of B at address_mat_b + k, byte j = B[k][j]; this
4	    // design's address strides reset to 1), the register block configured over APB, start_tpu written,
5	    // done_tpu polled, the 18 words around address_mat_c read back from BRAM A. A multiply writes its

5236	reg [3:0] state;
5237	
5238	`define STATE_INIT         4'b0000
5239	`define STATE_MATMUL       4'b0001
5240	`define STATE_NORM         4'b0010
5241	`define STATE_POOL         4'b0011
5242	`define STATE_ACTIVATION   4'b0100
5243	`define STATE_DONE         4'b0101
5244	
5245	//////////////////////////////////////////////////////
5246	// Assumption: We will always run matmul first. That is, matmul is not optional. 
5247	//             The other blocks - norm, act, pool - are optional.
5248	// Assumption: Order is fixed: Matmul -> Norm -> Pool -> Activation
5249	//////////////////////////////////////////////////////
5250	
5251	always @( posedge clk) begin
5252	    if (reset) begin
5253	      state <= `STATE_INIT;
5254	      start_mat_mul <= 1'b0;
5255	      done_tpu <= 1'b0;
5256	    end else begin
5257	      case (state)
5258	      `STATE_INIT: begin
5259	        if ((start_tpu == 1'b1) && (done_tpu == 1'b0)) begin
5260	          if (enable_matmul == 1'b1) begin
5261	            start_mat_mul <= 1'b1;
5262	            state <= `STATE_MATMUL;
5263	          end  
5264	        end  
5265	      end
5266	      
5267	      //start_mat_mul is kinda used as a reset in some logic
5268	      //inside the matmul unit. So, we can't make it 0 right away after
5269	      //asserting it.
5270	      `STATE_MATMUL: begin
5271	        if (done_mat_mul == 1'b1) begin
5272	            start_mat_mul <= 1'b0;
5273	            if(save_output_to_accum) begin
5274	              state <= `STATE_DONE;
5275	            end
5276	            else if (enable_norm) begin
5277	              state <= `STATE_NORM;
5278	            end 
5279	            else if (enable_pool) begin
5280	              state <= `STATE_POOL;
5281	            end
5282	            else if (enable_activation) begin
5283	              state <= `STATE_ACTIVATION;
5284	            end
5285	            else begin
5286	              state <= `STATE_DONE;
5287	            end  
5288	        end 
5289	        else begin
5290	          start_mat_mul <= 1'b1;	      
5291	        end
5292	      end      
5293	      
5294	      `STATE_NORM: begin                 
5295	        if (done_norm == 1'b1) begin
5296	          if (enable_pool) begin
5297	            state <= `STATE_POOL;
5298	          end
5299	          else if (enable_activation) begin
5300	            state <= `STATE_ACTIVATION;
5301	          end
5302	          else begin
5303	            state <= `STATE_DONE;
5304	          end
5305	        end
5306	      end
5307	
5308	      `STATE_POOL: begin                 
5309	        if (done_pool == 1'b1) begin
5310	          if (enable_activation) begin
5311	            state <= `STATE_ACTIVATION;
5312	          end
5313	          else begin
5314	            state <= `STATE_DONE;
5315	          end
5316	        end
5317	      end
5318	
5319	      `STATE_ACTIVATION: begin                 
5320	        if (done_activation == 1'b1) begin
5321	          state <= `STATE_DONE;
5322	        end
5323	      end
5324	
5325	      `STATE_DONE: begin
5326	        //We need to write start_tpu to 0 in the CFG block to get out of this state
5327	        if (start_tpu == 1'b0) begin
5328	          state <= `STATE_INIT;
5329	          done_tpu <= 0;
5330	        end
5331	        else begin
5332	          done_tpu <= 1;
5333	        end
5334	      end

prompt
Task

You are the plan reviewer. You did not write this plan. Check it against the design source, the block's contract and the correctness rules; approve it if it violates none.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mult_add_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19]
  • output ports: result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge unless that edge clears it, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the block's registers have no initial value. Each loads its input at every clock edge where reset is low, so, for a block whose inputs, chainin included, are defined, the result is defined from the second edge after power-up, with or without a reset. A rewrite may not rely on the result before then.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); scanin = 0 always (the operand scan chain is not used: ay is driven directly, and scanout is left unconnected); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of result (clock-to-Q)0.060
chainout: input register clock-to-Q + delay, to the output pin0.060 + 2.140 = 2.200
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

The design source is tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v. The candidate regions the planner was offered:

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • Every claim the plan makes about the design (who reads a value, a register's reset or enable, widths, cited lines, ASSUMES lines) against the source.
  • That the mapping as described keeps the same behavior.
  • That the plan states everything the planner's output section requires, listed below.

Do not judge cost or style; measurement decides cost.

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left: a correctness reason, or "not chosen this pass"; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output
  • First line exactly APPROVE or REVISE.
  • If REVISE, for each problem: the rule it violates or the required item it omits, by name; the design lines; what to change.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically, and your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The plan

I'll start by reading the design source and the reference files.

SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE (no block is chained; each block holds one product and, in activation, that product's own addend. The 4 norm products marked sum_in=top are summed in pool's window-4 adder at line 5392, downstream of norm's out_data port — that sum stays in soft logic.)
BLOCKS: 32
MAPPING:

  • 16 blocks in module activation (u_act_mul0 .. u_act_mul15), one per lane i = 0..15:

    • clk = clk
    • reset = act_blk_rst = reset | ~enable_activation | ~(in_data_available | activation_in_progress) | ~activation_type
    • mode_sigs = 12'b0, scanin = 19'b0, chainin = 64'b0
    • ax = {10'b0, inp_data_flopped[i*8 +: 8]} (18 bits)
    • ay = {11'b0, data_slope_flopped[i*8 +: 8]} (19 bits)
    • bx = {28'b0, data_intercept_flopped[i*8 +: 8]} (36 bits)
    • result[7:0] -> intercept_applied_data_internal[i*8 +: 8]; chainout and scanout left unconnected

  • 16 blocks in module norm (u_norm_mul0 .. u_norm_mul15), one per lane i = 0..15:

    • clk = clk
    • reset = norm_blk_rst = reset | ~enable_norm | ~(in_data_available | norm_in_progress)
    • mode_sigs = 12'b0, scanin = 19'b0, chainin = 64'b0, bx = 36'b0
    • ax = {10'b0, norm_ax_i} where wire [7:0] norm_ax_i = validity_mask[i] ? (inp_data[i*8 +: 8] - mean) : inp_data[i*8 +: 8];
    • ay = {11'b0, norm_ay_i} where wire [7:0] norm_ay_i = validity_mask[i] ? inv_var : 8'd1;
    • result[7:0] -> variance_applied_data[i*8 +: 8]; chainout and scanout left unconnected

PLAN:

ASSUMES: activation_type does not change while the activation datapath is active, i.e. while in_data_available | activation_in_progress is high (line 5477). It is a cfg register written only over APB (line 4913) with reset value 1 (line 4838); the software configures it before writing start_tpu (line 4885) and the control FSM only raises the activation phase after start (lines 5259-5323). Under this assumption the hold cases of lines 5482-5485 (active, activation_type==0) only ever hold the value 0, because every edge on which the block is not active writes 0 to those registers (lines 5467-5471 and 5522-5526); driving the hard block's synchronous reset instead therefore produces the same value.

ASSUMES: inv_var and validity_mask (= validity_mask_a_rows, line 5902) hold the same value on two consecutive clock edges while the norm datapath is active (line 5059). They are cfg registers written only over APB (lines 4889 and 4894, reset values at lines 4823 and 4829), and the software writes them before start_tpu (line 4885); the norm phase is entered only after start (lines 5259-5306). The rewrite samples them one edge earlier than lines 5068-5070 do, so it matches whenever they are steady across the pair of edges.

Both assumptions hold on the reference testbench's stimulus as well: the drive file configures 0x08/0x0a/0x3a/0x00 before each apb_write(8'h04, 32'h1) and only reads 0x04 while a run is in flight, and in the random phase address 0x04 is never written, so start_tpu stays 0, start_mat_mul stays 0, c_data_available is held at 0 (line 2305-2307) and both norm and activation sit in their zeroing branches regardless of the random writes to 0x0a/0x20/0x3a.

Why these regions, and why the others are left
  • qmult.$mul$...4728$349(sum_in=seq_mac), 256 instances — LEFT, capacity. One block of this mode occupies a whole DSP tile and the device has 132 of them; this region needs 256 blocks, so it cannot be placed. (A partial conversion is not offered: the region is one shape repeated 256 times and converting a subset would still need more than 132 tiles once combined with anything else, and converting a strict subset changes no arithmetic the region does not already do.) Note also that its sum is a 1-cycle accumulator loop (out_temp <= out_temp + mul_out_temp_reg, lines 4697-4704): the block's result is 2 cycles behind its operands and chainout may not reach general routing, so bx <= result feedback would give a 2-cycle accumulation loop and could not reproduce it anyway.
  • activation $470, $474, $478, $482, $486, $490, $494, $498, $502, $506, $510, $514, $518, $522, $526, $530 — CONVERTED. Each is one lane of lines 5482/5485: an 8x8 product truncated to 8 bits, added one stage later to data_intercept_delayed. Each becomes one block computing ax*ay + bx.
  • norm $368, $371, $374, $377, $380, $383, $386, $389, $392, $395, $398, $401, $404, $407, $410, $413 — CONVERTED. Each is one lane of line 5070 (and the mask-off pass-through of line 5074): one 8x8 product truncated to 8 bits. Each becomes one block computing ax*ay with bx = 0. The downstream adder chain in pool (line 5392) that the four sum_in=top ids refer to is not moved into any block: norm.out_data is a module port read by pool and by top, so it must stay visible.
Blocks added

32 blocks, 32 DSP tiles of the 132 available. No block is chained: every chainin is tied to 64'b0 and every chainout is left unconnected. Every scanin is 19'b0, every mode_sigs is 12'b0, scanout is unconnected. Do not copy the mult_add_int_18x19 model into the design file — instantiate it only.

Register stages, before and after
  • activation, each of the 16 regions. Before: 2 register stages from the block's operands to the sum — data_slope_flopped/inp_data_flopped/data_intercept_flopped (stage 0, the operands) -> slope_applied_data_internal and data_intercept_delayed (stage 1) -> intercept_applied_data_internal (stage 2). After: 2 stages, the block's own — operand registers ax_reg/ay_reg/bx_reg (stage 1) -> result (stage 2). The multiply is retimed one stage later, across slope_applied_data_internal: the product of two registered values is unchanged by registering the operands and multiplying a cycle later. Counting from the module input, inp_data -> out_data_internal is 3 stages before and 3 after.
  • norm, each of the 16 regions. Before: 2 register stages from inp_data to variance_applied_data — mean_applied_data (stage 1) -> variance_applied_data (stage 2). After: 2 stages — the block's ax_reg (stage 1, which is mean_applied_data) -> result (stage 2). The - mean / mask select stays in soft logic ahead of ax, exactly where line 5069/5073 put it.
Edits in module norm (lines 5007-5109)

  1. Delete the reg declarations of mean_applied_data and variance_applied_data (lines 5023-5024). Declare instead:

    • wire [63:0] norm_res0 .. norm_res15;
    • wire [DESIGN_SIZE*DWIDTH-1:0] variance_applied_data = {norm_res15[7:0], norm_res14[7:0], ..., norm_res1[7:0], norm_res0[7:0]}; (lane 15 at the top, lane 0 at the bottom, so that bits [i*8 +: 8] are lane i, as in the original).
    • wire norm_blk_rst = reset | ~enable_norm | ~(in_data_available | norm_in_progress);
    • 16 pairs of 8-bit wires, written out explicitly so the subtraction truncates to 8 bits exactly as line 5069 does:
      wire [7:0] norm_ax0 = validity_mask[0] ? (inp_data[0*8 +: 8] - mean) : inp_data[0*8 +: 8];
      wire [7:0] norm_ay0 = validity_mask[0] ? inv_var : 8'd1;
      ... through index 15. Do not inline the subtraction into the .ax(...) port expression: in an 18-bit context it would not wrap at 8 bits.
  2. In the always block: delete mean_applied_data <= 0; and variance_applied_data <= 0; at lines 5051-5052 and at 5098-5099, and delete the whole for loop of lines 5067-5076 (its entire body was those two registers). Leave cycle_count, out_data_available_internal, done_norm_internal, norm_in_progress, in_data_available_flopped and inp_data_flopped exactly as they are. reg [31:0] i; may stay declared and unused.
  3. Leave assign out_data_internal = variance_applied_data; (line 5107) as it is — it now reads the wire.
  4. Add the 16 instances, pattern (index 0 shown, repeat for 1..15):
    ```

    .result(act_res0), .chainout(), .scanout());
    ```
Why this is the same behavior

Write E for the block's reset expression and note that in both modules E is exactly the negation of the "update" branch condition of the original always block (plus ~activation_type in activation, covered by the first ASSUMES). For a register that the original writes with 0 on every edge where E is high, the block's synchronous clear reproduces it.

  • norm: the block's ax_reg after edge k is E(k) ? 0 : (mask ? inp_data-mean : inp_data) sampled just before edge k — bit for bit the original mean_applied_data (lines 5051/5069/5073/5098). result after edge k is E(k) ? 0 : ax_reg(k-1)*ay_reg(k-1), and ay_reg(k-1) is inv_var for a masked-in lane and 1 for a masked-out lane, so the product is mean_applied_data(k-1)*inv_var or mean_applied_data(k-1) — lines 5070/5074, with result[7:0] giving the same 8-bit truncation. bx and chainin are 0, so the 64-bit wrap is never reached. mean_applied_data has no other reader (only lines 5070 and 5074), so moving it inside the block hides nothing that is read elsewhere.
  • activation: ax_reg/ay_reg after edge k-1 hold inp_data_flopped/data_slope_flopped as of edge k-2, so the product formed for result(k) is exactly the product line 5482 forms for slope_applied_data_internal(k-1); bx_reg(k-1) holds data_intercept_flopped(k-2), which is exactly what data_intercept_delayed(k-1) holds (line 5484). Hence result(k)[7:0] = (product + intercept) mod 256, equal to the original (product mod 256 + intercept) mod 256 of lines 5482+5485. slope_applied_data_internal and data_intercept_delayed have no other readers (only line 5485), so they may live inside the block; inp_data_flopped does have another reader (line 5461) and is kept outside and fed into ax as well.
  • Power-up: the blocks' registers are X until the first clock edge that samples reset/E low... both E expressions include the top-level reset, which the testbench holds high for its first 4 cycles, so every block register is cleared at the first edge and result is defined from the first edge onward — well before the first compared cycle. No added soft register relies on an initial value: the only new state is inside the blocks.
  • Mid-run resets: E is re-derived combinationally from reset every cycle, so a reset pulse at any time clears the block exactly on the edge at which lines 5050/5466 clear the originals.

DESIGN_CHANGE: The 256 seq_mac MACs are where almost all of this design's multiplies are, and they are unreachable for two independent reasons — 256 blocks against 132 DSP tiles, and the 1-cycle accumulator loop at lines 4697-4704 against the block's 2-cycle operand-to-result latency with no general-routing path out of chainout. Restructuring seq_mac so the accumulation is a 2-cycle (even/odd interleaved) loop, and time-multiplexing the 16x16 array down to at most 132 physical MACs, would let that region use this block and would dominate any gain available from norm and activation.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_tpu_os_top.v) and the block's behavioural model (mult_add_int_18x19.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v

response

I'll read the design source and check the plan's claims.

APPROVE

Checked against /work/tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v and sim_reference/mult_add_int_18x19.v:

Widths / lanes — DWIDTH=8 (line 74), DESIGN_SIZE=16 (line 78), MASK_WIDTH=16 (line 81), so 16 lanes each in norm and activation matches the 32 converted ids. ax={10'b0,8}=18, ay={11'b0,8}=19, bx={28'b0,8}=36 all fit; max value 255·255+255 is far below 2^64, so the wrap never fires and result[7:0] reproduces the original 8-bit truncations.

Visibility — grep confirms mean_applied_data is read only at 5070/5074, slope_applied_data_internal only at 5485, data_intercept_delayed only at 5485. inp_data_flopped does have a second reader (5461) and the plan correctly keeps it outside. norm's out_data leaves the module (5107→5032→norm_data_out 5684/5900, consumed by u_pool 5915), so leaving the window-4 adder (5392) in soft logic is required and done.

Reset derivation — norm 5050/5059/5097 and activation 5466/5477/5520 give exactly the negated update conditions the plan uses. The retiming is sound at the clear boundaries too: when E(k)=1 the block zeroes ax_reg/ay_reg/bx_reg, whose product/sum is 0, matching the original's slope_applied_data_internal(k)=0 and data_intercept_delayed(k)=0; when E(k)=0 they reproduce 5482/5484 exactly. (a*b mod 256 + c) mod 256 == (a*b + c) mod 256, so folding 5482 and 5485 into one block result is exact.

ASSUMES citations — 4838 (activation_type reset 1), 4913 (only APB write), 4885 (start_tpu), 5259–5323 (FSM enters phases only after start), 4823/4829 (inv_var, validity_mask_a_rows reset values), 4889/4894 (only APB writes), 5902 (mask connection), 2305–2307 (c_data_available gated on start_mat_mul) all read as cited. Both assumptions are justified rather than merely asserted.

Stimulus — config_blocks (tasks line 60) writes 0x3a/0x08/0x0a before the enables word and before apb_write(8'h04,32'h1), and only apb_read(8'h04) runs during a run, so activation_type, inv_var and validity_mask are constant across runs 2 and 3. Run 1 (drive line 12) never calls config_blocks, but it writes enables=32'h1, leaving enable_norm/enable_activation at 0, so both blocks sit in their clearing branch and the assumptions are vacuous there — the plan's phrasing "before each apb_write(8'h04, 32'h1)" is loose on run 1 but the conclusion holds. In the random phase PADDR==8'h04 is remapped to 8'h00 (drive line 38), so start_tpu stays 0, start_mat_mul stays 0, c_data_available stays 0 (2305–2307), and norm/activation remain in their zeroing branches with E=1, so the random writes to 0x00/0x0a/0x20/0x3a are harmless. Random reset pulses (drive line 45) appear in both E expressions and clear on the same edge as 5050/5466.

Power-up — reset reaches both modules from top (5905, 5938) and is in both E expressions; the testbench's 4-cycle reset phase clears every block register at the first edge, so nothing relies on an initial value.

Left region — qmult...$349(sum_in=seq_mac) is left with both a capacity reason (256 > 132 DSP tiles) and a correctness reason verified at 4689–4704: out_temp <= out_temp + mul_out_temp_reg is a 1-cycle accumulator loop, unreachable with a 2-cycle operand-to-result block whose chainout cannot enter general routing.

Required items — all 33 candidates accounted for (32 converted, 1 left with reason), block count stated (32), and register stages before/after given for both converted groups (2 → 2 in each, no latency change).

1	        // tpu_like.small.os.fixed at its top: the ws drive sequence on the os register map and its stride-16
2	        // layout. Run 1 matmul alone; the APB write that pulses pe_reset (the design's own one-cycle pulse,
3	        // with the array quiet: the protocol); a full reset; run 2 every block; a full reset; run 3 matmul
4	        // and norm; the random phase (APB traffic on the register set except the start/done word, BRAM
5	        // writes, reset 1 in 1024). Under START_ONLY the full resets and the random resets are left out.
6	        // Under PE_RESET_IN_FLIGHT (the diagnostic) one more APB write of the pe_reset bit lands while run
7	        // 2's multiply is in flight, which the protocol excludes.
8	        fill_matrices;
9	        phase = "run1";
10	        load_matrices(0, 10'd0, 10'd0);
11	        apb_write(8'h0e, 32'd0); apb_write(8'h12, 32'd0); apb_write(8'h16, 32'd768);
12	        apb_write(8'h00, 32'h1); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h1);
13	        wait_done(400); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h0);
14	        phase = "readback1"; read_c(10'd768, "run 1 (matmul)");
15	        phase = "pe_reset"; apb_write(8'h04, 32'h8000);   // the pe_reset bit, array quiet
16	        zero_cycles(4);
17	        if (`MIDRUN_RESETS) begin phase = "reset-pulse"; full_reset; end
18	        phase = "run2";
19	        load_matrices(1, 10'd256, 10'd256);
20	        apb_write(8'h0e, 32'd256); apb_write(8'h12, 32'd256); apb_write(8'h16, 32'd768);
21	        config_blocks(32'hf); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h1);
22	`ifdef PE_RESET_IN_FLIGHT
23	        zero_cycles(30); phase = "pe_reset-live"; apb_write(8'h04, 32'h8000);   // a one-cycle pe_reset with products in flight
24	`endif
25	        wait_done(400); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h0);
26	        phase = "readback2"; read_c(10'd768, "run 2 (every block)");
27	        if (`MIDRUN_RESETS) begin phase = "reset-pulse"; full_reset; end
28	        phase = "run3";
29	        load_matrices(2, 10'd512, 10'd512);
30	        apb_write(8'h0e, 32'd512); apb_write(8'h12, 32'd512); apb_write(8'h16, 32'd768);
31	        config_blocks(32'h3); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h1);
32	        wait_done(400); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h0);
33	        phase = "readback3"; read_c(10'd768, "run 3 (matmul and norm, after reset)");
34	        phase = "random"; ph = 0;
35	        for (i = 0; i < `RANDOM_CYCLES; i = i + 1) begin
36	            tick; set_data;
37	            case (ph)
38	                0: begin PSEL = 1; draw; PWRITE = r32[0]; PADDR = reg_addr(r32[5:1]); if (PADDR == 8'h04) PADDR = 8'h00; PENABLE = 0; apb_addr = PADDR; apb_write_q = PWRITE; apb_wdata = PWDATA; end   // no random start or pe_reset write: the protocol
39	                1: begin PSEL = 1; PENABLE = 1; PADDR = apb_addr; PWRITE = apb_write_q; PWDATA = apb_wdata; end   // set_data redrew them; the access cycle keeps the setup cycle's (APB: stable through setup and access)
40	                2: begin PSEL = 0; PENABLE = 0; PWRITE = 0; end
41	                default: begin PSEL = 0; PENABLE = 0; end
42	            endcase
43	            ph = (ph + 1) % 4;
44	            draw; bram_we_a_ext = (r32[1:0] == 0) ? 16'hffff : 16'h0; bram_we_b_ext = (r32[3:2] == 0) ? 16'hffff : 16'h0;
45	            draw; reset = (`MIDRUN_RESETS && (r32 % 1024) == 0); resetn = ~reset;
46	        end
47	        PSEL = 0; PENABLE = 0; PWRITE = 0; bram_we_a_ext = 0; bram_we_b_ext = 0;

1	    // tpu_like.small.ws top (from the hand-written testbench of 2026-10-19): the design driven as its
2	    // software drives it. Matrices written into the two BRAMs through the external ports (word k of A at
3	    // address_mat_a + k, byte i = A[i][k]; word k of B at address_mat_b + k, byte j = B[k][j]; this
4	    // design's address strides reset to 1), the register block configured over APB, start_tpu written,
5	    // done_tpu polled, the 18 words around address_mat_c read back from BRAM A. A multiply writes its
6	    // results once per reset (the accumulator's start_pooling is cleared by resetn alone, the activation
7	    // unit's done count by reset alone), so every run after the first follows a 4-cycle reset of both.
8	    reg [7:0] MA [0:767]; reg [7:0] MB [0:767];
9	    reg [31:0] rd; reg [127:0] wv; integer polls, nz_c, ii, jj, kk, ph;
10	    reg [7:0] apb_addr; reg apb_write_q; reg [31:0] apb_wdata;   // the random phase's setup-cycle address, direction and write data, held through its access cycle
11	    task fill_matrices; integer q; begin
12	        for (q = 0; q < 768; q = q + 1) begin draw; MA[q] = r32[7:0]; end
13	        for (q = 0; q < 768; q = q + 1) begin draw; MB[q] = r32[7:0]; end
14	    end endtask
15	    task apb_write; input [7:0] a; input [31:0] d; begin
16	        tick; PSEL = 1; PWRITE = 1; PADDR = a; PWDATA = d; PENABLE = 0;
17	        tick; PENABLE = 1;
18	        tick; PSEL = 0; PENABLE = 0; PWRITE = 0;
19	        tick;
20	    end endtask
21	    task apb_read; input [7:0] a; begin
22	        tick; PSEL = 1; PWRITE = 0; PADDR = a; PENABLE = 0;
23	        tick; PENABLE = 1;
24	        tick; rd = PRDATA_ref; PSEL = 0; PENABLE = 0;
25	        tick;
26	    end endtask
27	    task bram_write_a; input [9:0] a; input [127:0] d; begin
28	        tick; bram_addr_a_ext = a; bram_wdata_a_ext = d; bram_we_a_ext = 16'hffff;
29	        tick; bram_we_a_ext = 0;
30	    end endtask
31	    task bram_write_b; input [9:0] a; input [127:0] d; begin
32	        tick; bram_addr_b_ext = a; bram_wdata_b_ext = d; bram_we_b_ext = 16'hffff;
33	        tick; bram_we_b_ext = 0;
34	    end endtask
35	    task load_matrices; input integer m; input [9:0] base_a, base_b; begin
36	        for (kk = 0; kk < 16; kk = kk + 1) begin
37	            for (ii = 0; ii < 16; ii = ii + 1) wv[ii*8 +: 8] = MA[m*256 + ii*16 + kk];
38	            bram_write_a(base_a + 16 * kk, wv);
39	        end
40	        for (kk = 0; kk < 16; kk = kk + 1) begin
41	            for (jj = 0; jj < 16; jj = jj + 1) wv[jj*8 +: 8] = MB[m*256 + kk*16 + jj];
42	            bram_write_b(base_b + 16 * kk, wv);
43	        end
44	    end endtask
45	    task wait_done; input integer maxpolls; begin
46	        polls = 0; rd = 0;
47	        while (rd[31] == 0 && polls < maxpolls) begin apb_read(8'h04); polls = polls + 1; end
48	        if (rd[31]) $display("  t=%0t  done_tpu read as 1 over APB after %0d polls", $time, polls);
49	        else        $display("  t=%0t  done_tpu not read as 1 within %0d polls", $time, maxpolls);
50	    end endtask
51	    task read_c; input [9:0] cbase; input [8*40-1:0] what; begin   // the 18 words from address_mat_c + 16 downward by the stride 16
52	        nz_c = 0;
53	        for (jj = 0; jj < 18; jj = jj + 1) begin
54	            tick; bram_addr_a_ext = cbase + 16 - 16 * jj; bram_we_a_ext = 0;
55	            tick; wv = bram_rdata_a_ext_ref; for (ii = 0; ii < 16; ii = ii + 1) if (wv[ii*8 +: 8] != 0) nz_c = nz_c + 1;
56	        end
57	        $display("  result %0s: 18 words read back from BRAM A at %0d down to %0d by 16, %0d of 288 bytes nonzero on the reference", what, cbase + 16, cbase - 256, nz_c);
58	    end endtask

61	        apb_write(8'h00, enables);
62	    end endtask

1	// mult_add_int_18x19: result = ax*ay + bx + chainin, unsigned, the product and bx zero-extended
2	// to 64 bits, modulo 2^64 (the sum wraps at the result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19] in;
4	// result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19] out. mode_sigs and scanin are not read (the scan chain is
5	// unused and tied to 0 in every spec).
6	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
7	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx and chainin alike); the product and
8	// the sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
9	// Cycles: ax, ay, bx and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
10	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (1 cycle from the
11	// inputs).
12	// scanout is the ay register (ay to scanout 1), as before; nothing specifies it.
13	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
14	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
15	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
16	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
17	// X until the first clear.
18	module mult_add_int_18x19 (
19	    input clk,
20	    input reset,
21	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
22	    input [17:0] ax,
23	    input [18:0] ay,
24	    input [35:0] bx,
25	    input [63:0] chainin,
26	    input [18:0] scanin,
27	    output reg [63:0] result,
28	    output [63:0] chainout,
29	    output [18:0] scanout
30	);
31	
32	    // stage 1: the input registers
33	    reg [17:0] ax_reg;
34	    reg [18:0] ay_reg;
35	    reg [35:0] bx_reg;
36	    reg [63:0] chainin_reg;
37	
38	    // combinational middle: the product, the addend and chainin, from the registers
39	    wire [36:0] prod = ax_reg * ay_reg;                          // 18x19 -> 37, exact
40	    wire [63:0] sum  = prod + bx_reg + chainin_reg;              // zero-extended, modulo 2^64
41	
42	    always @(posedge clk) begin
43	        if (reset) begin
44	            result <= 0;
45	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
46	        end
47	        else begin
48	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; chainin_reg <= chainin;
49	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
50	        end
51	    end
52	
53	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
54	    assign scanout  = ay_reg;
55	
56	endmodule
