module norm(
    input enable_norm,
    input [`DWIDTH-1:0] mean,
    input [`DWIDTH-1:0] inv_var,
    input in_data_available,
    input [`DESIGN_SIZE*`DWIDTH-1:0] inp_data,
    output [`DESIGN_SIZE*`DWIDTH-1:0] out_data,
    output out_data_available,
    input [`MASK_WIDTH-1:0] validity_mask,
    output done_norm,
    input clk,
    input reset
);

reg out_data_available_internal;
wire [`DESIGN_SIZE*`DWIDTH-1:0] out_data_internal;
reg [`DESIGN_SIZE*`DWIDTH-1:0] mean_applied_data;
reg [`DESIGN_SIZE*`DWIDTH-1:0] variance_applied_data;
reg done_norm_internal;
reg norm_in_progress;
reg in_data_available_flopped;
reg [`DESIGN_SIZE*`DWIDTH-1:0] inp_data_flopped;

//Muxing logic to handle the case when this block is disabled
assign out_data_available = (enable_norm) ? out_data_available_internal : in_data_available_flopped;
assign out_data = (enable_norm) ? out_data_internal : inp_data_flopped;
assign done_norm = (enable_norm) ? done_norm_internal : 1'b1;

//inp_data will have multiple elements in it. the number of elements is the same as size of the matmul.
//on each clock edge, if in_data_available is 1, then we will normalize the inputs.

//the code uses the funky part-select syntax. example:
//wire [7:0] byteN = word[byte_num*8 +: 8];
//byte_num*8 is the starting point. 8 is the width is the part-select (has to be constant).in_data_available
//+: indicates the part-select increases from the starting point
//-: indicates the part-select decreases from the starting point
//another example:
//loc = 3;
//PA[loc -:4] = PA[loc+1 +:4];  // equivalent to PA[3:0] = PA[7:4];

reg [31:0] cycle_count;
reg [31:0] i;
always @(posedge clk) begin
    if ((reset || ~enable_norm)) begin
        mean_applied_data <= 0;
        variance_applied_data <= 0;
        out_data_available_internal <= 0;
        cycle_count <= 0;
        done_norm_internal <= 0;
        norm_in_progress <= 0;
        in_data_available_flopped <= in_data_available;
        inp_data_flopped <= inp_data;
    end else if (in_data_available || norm_in_progress) begin
        cycle_count <= cycle_count + 1;
        //Let's apply mean and variance as the input data comes in.
        //We have a pipeline here. First stage does the add (to apply the mean)
        //and second stage does the multiplication (to apply the variance).
        //Note: the following loop is not a loop across multiple columns of data.
        //This loop will run in 2 cycle on the same column of data that comes into 
        //this module in 1 clock.
        for (i = 0; i < `DESIGN_SIZE; i=i+1) begin
            if (validity_mask[i] == 1'b1) begin
                mean_applied_data[i*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH] <= (inp_data[i*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH] - mean);
                variance_applied_data[i*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH] <= (mean_applied_data[i*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH] * inv_var);
            end 
            else begin
                mean_applied_data[i*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH] <= (inp_data[i*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH]);
                variance_applied_data[i*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH] <= (mean_applied_data[i*`DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH]);
            end
        end

        //Out data is available starting with the second clock cycle because 
        //in the first cycle, we only apply the mean.
        if(cycle_count==2) begin
            out_data_available_internal <= 1;
        end

        //When we've normalized values N times, where N is the matmul
        //size, that means we're done. But there is one additional cycle
        //that is taken in the beginning (when we are applying the mean to the first
        //column of data). We can call this the Initiation Interval of the pipeline.
        //So, for a 4x4 matmul, this block takes 5 cycles.
        if(cycle_count==(`DESIGN_SIZE+1)) begin
            done_norm_internal <= 1'b1;
            norm_in_progress <= 0;
        end
        else begin
            nor

Task

You are the acceptance reviewer. You did not plan or write this edit. Check the edit against the approved plan, the block's contract and the correctness rules.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mult_add_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19]
  • output ports: result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge unless that edge clears it, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the block's registers have no initial value. Each loads its input at every clock edge where reset is low, so, for a block whose inputs, chainin included, are defined, the result is defined from the second edge after power-up, with or without a reset. A rewrite may not rely on the result before then.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); scanin = 0 always (the operand scan chain is not used: ay is driven directly, and scanout is left unconnected); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

The file tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v is the edited design. The edit is the diff below, taken against the original source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.

  • The edit does what the approved plan says; any change outside the regions it names is explained by the plan.
  • Every block input port is connected, and the block's outputs drive what the plan says they drive.
  • The correctness rules hold for the edited source.

The synthesis checks and the reference simulation have passed; the remaining checks run after your review.

SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE (no block is chained; each block holds one product and, in activation, that product's own addend. The 4 norm products marked sum_in=top are summed in pool's window-4 adder at line 5392, downstream of norm's out_data port — that sum stays in soft logic.)
BLOCKS: 32
MAPPING:

  • 16 blocks in module activation (u_act_mul0 .. u_act_mul15), one per lane i = 0..15:

    • clk = clk
    • reset = act_blk_rst = ~act_g, with wire act_g = ~reset & enable_activation & (in_data_available | activation_in_progress) & activation_type; (same condition as the first pass's reset | ~enable_activation | ~(in_data_available | activation_in_progress) | ~activation_type)
    • mode_sigs = 12'b0, scanin = 19'b0, chainin = 64'b0
    • ax = {10'b0, inp_data_flopped[i*8 +: 8]} (18 bits)
    • ay = {11'b0, data_slope[i*8 +: 8]} (19 bits) — data_slope is now the slope LUT read with the new register addr_q, not with address
    • bx = {28'b0, data_intercept[i*8 +: 8]} (36 bits) — data_intercept is now the intercept LUT read with addr_q and gated by the new 1-bit register act_g_q
    • result[7:0] -> intercept_applied_data_internal[i*8 +: 8]; chainout and scanout left unconnected

  • 16 blocks in module norm (u_norm_mul0 .. u_norm_mul15), one per lane i = 0..15 — unchanged from the first pass:

    • clk = clk
    • reset = norm_blk_rst = reset | ~enable_norm | ~(in_data_available | norm_in_progress)
    • mode_sigs = 12'b0, scanin = 19'b0, chainin = 64'b0, bx = 36'b0
    • ax = {10'b0, norm_axi} where wire [7:0] norm_axi = validity_mask[i] ? (inp_data[i*8 +: 8] - mean) : inp_data[i*8 +: 8];
    • ay = {11'b0, norm_ayi} where wire [7:0] norm_ayi = validity_mask[i] ? inv_var : 8'd1;
    • result[7:0] -> variance_applied_data[i*8 +: 8]; chainout and scanout left unconnected

PLAN:

This is the first pass's rewrite with the activation soft pipeline re-cut. The first pass measured A/A0 1.054, D/D0 0.959, product 1.011. Both levers are addressed by one change, and norm is left exactly as it was measured:

  • The 1-cycle soft stage that feeds the blocks in activation is moved from after the slope/intercept LUTs to before them: the two 128-bit registers data_slope_flopped and data_intercept_flopped are replaced by one 64-bit register addr_q holding the LUT address, plus one 1-bit register act_g_q. That removes 191 flip-flops of soft logic on top of the 640 the first pass already removed.
  • The path VPR reported as critical at seed 1 ends at $sdff~1363^Q~4 after five .names levels fed from a .latch, with 4.8 ns of its 5.73 ns in wire. That is the shape of pool.out_data_temp -> enable_pool mux -> activation's address comparators (5580-5619) -> slope/intercept LUT (5540-5577) -> data_slope_flopped. Cutting the register one level earlier (at address instead of at data_slope/data_intercept) takes the final LUT level, and the 1.908 ns wire that feeds it, off that register-to-register path; the LUT level moves into the next stage, which is only LUT -> block input register (setup 0.019). If that path was indeed the critical one, the period drops below the 5.786 ns the first pass reached; if it was not, the edit is still an area reduction of about 1% at the same period.

ASSUMES: activation_type does not change while the activation datapath is active, i.e. while in_data_available | activation_in_progress is high (line 5477). It is a cfg register written only over APB (line 4913) with reset value 1 (line 4838); software configures it before writing start_tpu (line 4885) and the control FSM only raises the activation phase after start (lines 5259-5323). Under this assumption the hold cases of lines 5482-5485 (active, activation_type==0) only ever hold 0, because every edge on which the block is not active writes 0 to those registers (lines 5467-5471 and 5522-5526); and when activation_type is steadily 0 the whole tanh datapath is invisible anyway, since line 5534 then selects relu_applied_data_internal. Driving the hard block's synchronous reset with ~act_g therefore produces the same visible value.

ASSUMES: inv_var and validity_mask (= validity_mask_a_rows, line 5902) hold the same value on two consecutive clock edges while the norm datapath is active (line 5059). They are cfg registers written only over APB (lines 4889 and 4894, reset values at lines 4823 and 4829), written before start_tpu (line 4885); the norm phase is entered only after start (lines 5259-5306). The rewrite samples them one edge earlier than lines 5068-5070 do, so it matches whenever they are steady across the pair of edges.

Both assumptions hold on the reference testbench's stimulus: the drive file configures 0x08/0x0a/0x3a/0x00 before each apb_write(8'h04, 32'h1) and only reads 0x04 while a run is in flight; in the random phase address 0x04 is never written, so start_tpu and start_mat_mul stay 0, c_data_available is held at 0 (lines 2305-2307), and both norm and activation sit in their zeroing branches regardless of the random writes to 0x0a/0x20/0x3a. The first pass, which rests on the same two assumptions, passed the reference simulation.

Every candidate region, converted or left
  • activation $470, $474, $478, $482, $486, $490, $494, $498, $502, $506, $510, $514, $518, $522, $526, $530 — CONVERTED (one block per lane: ax*ay + bx).
  • norm $368, $371, $374, $377, $380, $383, $386, $389, $392, $395, $398, $401, $404, $407, $410, $413 — CONVERTED (one block per lane: ax*ay, bx tied to 0). The downstream adder chain in pool (line 5392) that the four sum_in=top ids refer to is not moved into any block: norm.out_data is a module port read by pool and by top, so it must stay visible, and it stays in soft logic.
  • qmult.$mul$...4728$349(sum_in=seq_mac), 256 instances — LEFT, correctness/capacity. One block of this mode takes a whole DSP tile and the device has 132; this region needs 256 blocks. Independently, its sum is a 1-cycle accumulator loop (out_temp <= out_temp + mul_out_temp_reg, lines 4697-4704): the block's result is 2 cycles behind its operands and chainout may not reach general routing, so a bx <= result feedback would be a 2-cycle accumulation and could not reproduce it.
Blocks added

32 blocks, 32 DSP tiles of the 132 available. No block is chained: every chainin is 64'b0 and every chainout is left unconnected. Every scanin is 19'b0, every mode_sigs is 12'b0, scanout unconnected. Do not copy the mult_add_int_18x19 model into the design file — instantiate it only.

Register stages, before and after
  • activation, each of the 16 regions. Before: 2 register stages from the multiply's operands to the sum — inp_data_flopped/data_slope_flopped/data_intercept_flopped (stage 0) -> slope_applied_data_internal and data_intercept_delayed (stage 1) -> intercept_applied_data_internal (stage 2). After: 2 stages, the block's own — operand registers ax_reg/ay_reg/bx_reg (stage 1) -> result (stage 2). Counting from the module input, inp_data -> out_data_internal is 3 stages before (inp_data_flopped, slope_applied, intercept_applied) and 3 after (inp_data_flopped/addr_q, block operand regs, block result). The LUT address path keeps exactly one register stage between inp_data and the multiplier operand: before it was inp_data -> address -> data_slope -> [data_slope_flopped] -> mult; after it is inp_data -> address -> [addr_q] -> data_slope -> mult.
  • norm, each of the 16 regions. Before: 2 stages from inp_data to variance_applied_data — mean_applied_data (stage 1) -> variance_applied_data (stage 2). After: 2 stages — the block's ax_reg (stage 1, which is mean_applied_data) -> result (stage 2). The - mean / mask select stays in soft logic ahead of ax, where lines 5069/5073 put it.

Why this is the same behavior

Write R_k for the content of register R after clock edge k, and s_k for the value of a combinational signal s during the cycle that ends at edge k+1 (so a register loaded at edge k+1 takes s_k). Let G_k = act_g_k = ~reset & enable_activation & (in_data_available|activation_in_progress) & activation_type during that cycle; G is exactly the condition of the update branch at 5477 intersected with the tanh branch at 5481.

Original (lines 5450-5457, 5465-5532), per lane, under the type assumption:

  • inp_data_flopped_{k+1} = reset_k ? 0 : inp_data_k
  • data_slope_flopped_{k+1} = reset_k ? 0 : f_s(address_k), f_s the slope LUT
  • data_intercept_flopped_{k+1} = G_k ? f_i(address_k) : 0, f_i the intercept LUT
  • data_intercept_delayed_{k+1} = G_k ? data_intercept_flopped_k : 0
  • slope_applied_{k+1} = G_k ? data_slope_flopped_k * inp_data_flopped_k : 0
  • intercept_applied_{k+1} = G_k ? (slope_applied_k + data_intercept_delayed_k) : 0

so intercept_applied_{k+1} = G_k ? (G_{k-1} ? (dsf_{k-1}*ipf_{k-1} + dif_{k-1}) : 0) : 0.

Block, with reset = ~G: the operand registers load at edge k under G_{k-1}, the result at edge k+1 under G_k, giving result_{k+1} = G_k ? (G_{k-1} ? (ax_{k-1}*ay_{k-1} + bx_{k-1}) : 0) : 0. So it suffices that ax_{k-1}=ipf_{k-1}, ay_{k-1}=dsf_{k-1}, bx_{k-1}=dif_{k-1} (mod 256 on the 8 bits read out):

  • ax = inp_data_flopped — the same register, kept (it is also read by line 5461, so it must stay outside the block anyway). Exact.
  • ay = f_s(addr_q): addr_q_k = reset_{k-1} ? 4'b0101 : address_{k-1}. If reset_{k-1}=0, f_s(addr_q_k) = f_s(address_{k-1}) = dsf_k. If reset_{k-1}=1, dsf_k = 0 and f_s(4'b0101) = 8'd0 (line 5548). Exact in both cases.
  • bx = act_g_q ? f_i(addr_q) : 0: act_g_q_k = G_{k-1}. When G_{k-1}=1, reset_{k-1}=0, so addr_q_k = address_{k-1} and bx_k = f_i(address_{k-1}) = dif_k; when G_{k-1}=0, bx_k = 0 = dif_k. Exact. (The gate is what reproduces the original's 0 intercept on the first active edge, when data_intercept_flopped was still cleared.)
  • The 64-bit wrap is never reached: the product is at most 255*255 and bx at most 255, and only result[7:0] is read, so (prod + intercept) mod 256 equals the original (prod mod 256 + intercept) mod 256 of lines 5482+5485.
  • Visibility: data_slope_flopped, data_intercept_flopped, data_intercept_delayed, slope_applied_data_internal have no reader outside lines 5482-5485 (checked across the module), so they may be removed or moved inside the blocks; address, data_slope, data_intercept stay in soft logic and keep driving the block pins; inp_data_flopped stays outside because line 5461 reads it.
  • norm: unchanged from the first pass, which matched on the reference testbench. ax_reg after edge k is norm_blk_rst ? 0 : (mask ? inp_data-mean : inp_data) — bit for bit the original mean_applied_data (lines 5051/5069/5073/5098); result after edge k is mean_applied_data(k-1)*inv_var for a masked-in lane and mean_applied_data(k-1) for a masked-out one (ay = 1), matching lines 5070/5074 with the same 8-bit truncation. mean_applied_data has no other reader.
  • Power-up and mid-run reset: addr_q and act_g_q are cleared by the top-level reset, which the testbench holds for its first 4 cycles, and act_blk_rst/norm_blk_rst are high throughout that window, so every block register is cleared at the first edge and every block result is defined from then on — well before the first compared cycle, and no added register's initial value is relied on. Both block reset expressions are re-derived combinationally from reset every cycle, so a reset pulse at any time clears the blocks on exactly the edges at which lines 5050/5466 clear the originals.

DESIGN_CHANGE: The 256 seq_mac MACs hold almost all of this design's multiplies and are unreachable for two independent reasons — 256 blocks against 132 DSP tiles, and the 1-cycle accumulator loop at lines 4697-4704 against the block's 2-cycle operand-to-result latency with no general-routing path out of chainout. Restructuring seq_mac so the accumulation is a 2-cycle (even/odd interleaved) loop, and time-multiplexing the 16x16 array down to at most 132 physical MACs, would let that region use this block and would dominate any gain available from norm and activation. Separately, activation's address decoder (lines 5580-5619, an 11-way priority comparison on 8 bits) is the longest soft path left in the datapath; splitting it across two pipeline stages would cut the period further, but that changes the module's latency and so is outside this rewrite.

simulation passed: 4577 cycles, 0 mismatches. The result (sim_result.json) and log (sim_run.log) are attached. Read these files in this directory: tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v, sim_result.json, sim_run.log

response

I'll read the source and supporting files before deciding.

ACCEPT

Checked the edited source (tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v), the block model in sim_reference/mult_add_int_18x19.v, and the sim artifacts.

Plan conformance

  • Only norm (5021-5241) and activation (5563-5896) are touched; the diff contains nothing outside those two modules, and the block model is not copied into the design (no module mult_add_int_18x19 in the file).
  • norm: mean_applied_data/variance_applied_data regs and the 5067-5076 loop deleted, variance_applied_data rebuilt as a concatenation wire (lane 15 at top, 5042-5046), norm_blk_rst = reset | ~enable_norm | ~(in_data_available | norm_in_progress) (5126), 16 explicit 8-bit norm_ax*/norm_ay* wires so the - mean truncates at 8 bits (5128-5159), assign out_data_internal = variance_applied_data; kept (5119).
  • activation: slope_applied_data_internal, data_slope_flopped, data_intercept_flopped, data_intercept_delayed removed; intercept_applied_data_internal is now a wire from the block results (5606-5610); addr_q/act_g_q added with the specified reset value {16{4'b0101}} (5612-5622); both LUTs moved to always @(*) on addr_q (5693, 5713) with the act_g_q gate on the intercept (5715-5717); the main always block keeps only the ReLU half (5642-5646) and the untouched cycle_count/done logic; the address decoder (5737-5776) and line 5687 unchanged.

Ports — all 32 instances (16 u_norm_mul0..15, 16 u_act_mul0..15, lane indices consistent) connect every input: clk, reset, mode_sigs(12'b0), ax (18 b), ay (19 b), bx (36 b / 36'b0 in norm), chainin(64'b0), scanin(19'b0); result drives only the per-lane concatenation, chainout()/scanout() unconnected. 32 blocks ≤ 132 DSP tiles; log confirms 32 instantiations.

Correctness

  • norm: block reset is exactly the complement of the original's active branch, so ax_reg reproduces mean_applied_data bit-for-bit and result[7:0] reproduces variance_applied_data (masked-out lane via ay = 1). The one-edge-early sampling of inv_var/validity_mask is the stated ASSUMES; on the first active edge both operand regs are 0 so no divergence there.
  • activation: with block reset ~act_g, result_{k+1} = G_k ? (G_{k-1} ? ax·ay+bx : 0) : 0, matching the original's G_k ? (G_{k-1} ? dsf·ipf + dif : 0) : 0. f_s(4'b0101)=0 (5701) and act_g_q=0 after reset cover the reset_{k-2}=1, reset_{k-1}=0 edge case; the stale data_slope_flopped value the original kept while inactive is never observable because the next stage was gated on the same condition. Only result[7:0] is read, and (prod + bx) mod 256 = (prod mod 256 + bx) mod 256, so the 8-bit truncation of lines 5482/5485 is preserved; negative intercepts carry as their 8-bit two's-complement pattern zero-extended into bx, same residue.
  • Visibility: inp_data_flopped stays in soft logic (read at 5625); grep shows no remaining reference to any of the four removed activation signals or to mean_applied_data, so none had an outside reader; data_slope/data_intercept/address stay outside and only drive block pins.
  • Reset/power-up: both block-reset expressions are re-derived from reset each cycle, so mid-run resets clear the blocks on exactly the edges the originals were cleared; addr_q/act_g_q are cleared by the top-level reset, and x_count: 0 with midrun_resets: 1 in the sim result is consistent.
  • The two ASSUMES: lines are stated with design-line citations, and the simulation (4577 cycles, 0 mismatches, 0 X) shows the testbench stimulus is matched regardless.

 "file_path": "/work/sim_reference/mult_add_int_18x19.v"

output
1	// mult_add_int_18x19: result = ax*ay + bx + chainin, unsigned, the product and bx zero-extended
2	// to 64 bits, modulo 2^64 (the sum wraps at the result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19] in;
4	// result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19] out. mode_sigs and scanin are not read (the scan chain is
5	// unused and tied to 0 in every spec).
6	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
7	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx and chainin alike); the product and
8	// the sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
9	// Cycles: ax, ay, bx and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
10	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (1 cycle from the
11	// inputs).
12	// scanout is the ay register (ay to scanout 1), as before; nothing specifies it.
13	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
14	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
15	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
16	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
17	// X until the first clear.
18	module mult_add_int_18x19 (
19	    input clk,
20	    input reset,
21	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
22	    input [17:0] ax,
23	    input [18:0] ay,
24	    input [35:0] bx,
25	    input [63:0] chainin,
26	    input [18:0] scanin,
27	    output reg [63:0] result,
28	    output [63:0] chainout,
29	    output [18:0] scanout
30	);
31	
32	    // stage 1: the input registers
33	    reg [17:0] ax_reg;
34	    reg [18:0] ay_reg;
35	    reg [35:0] bx_reg;
36	    reg [63:0] chainin_reg;
37	
38	    // combinational middle: the product, the addend and chainin, from the registers
39	    wire [36:0] prod = ax_reg * ay_reg;                          // 18x19 -> 37, exact
40	    wire [63:0] sum  = prod + bx_reg + chainin_reg;              // zero-extended, modulo 2^64
41	
42	    always @(posedge clk) begin
43	        if (reset) begin
44	            result <= 0;
45	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
46	        end
47	        else begin
48	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; chainin_reg <= chainin;
49	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
50	        end
51	    end
52	
53	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
54	    assign scanout  = ay_reg;
55	
56	endmodule

Objective loop

2 rewrite(s) measured · stopped by: no rewrite beats the original

seeds per rewrite

loop stopped by
no rewrite beats the original (Pass 2 -9.71% against the original, which is the best so far; a rewrite replaces the original only when its product is strictly below it)
objective
area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02)
baseline
layout koios_extra_small · used area 6.2834e+07 · critical path 6.03149 ns · wirelength 395418 · area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02) 3.78983e+08 (read from entry; median per term over the entry's seed runs (its seed_medians; seeds lists them))

The objective is area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02): A times D, from VPR's total used logic block area (I/Os and empty grid tiles excluded), A, the delay D (the register-to-register figure since 2026-10-20) and the total routed wirelength, W (all three recorded under every objective). Each term is the median over the loop's seeds; the objective is formed from the medians. Beside every product stand the ratios against the baseline, A/A0 (area), D/D0 (critical path), W/W0 (wirelength) and their product, so the normalization can be read off. After each measured rewrite, the planner is given every measurement so far and asked for a revision. A revision is built and checked like the first rewrite, and the lowest product is kept, a later rewrite having to beat it by more than 3.45%. The loop stops when the planner declines to revise, when a revision does not lower the best product by more than 3.45%, when a revision fails its checks, or when 9 revisions have been made.

Stated: the BLOCKS, TILES and STAGE_NS lines of the plan header. Measured: the packed DSP tile count from the gate flow, and the median period, area and wirelength over the seeds. "A/A0 · D/D0 · W/W0" are the rewrite's medians over the baseline's, whose product is the "vs baseline" change plus one. "vs previous" is against the rewrite measured before this one. "Path 1 ends in" is read from the rewrite's own timing report and netlist: the constant coefficients on the block (or plain multiply) that path 1 passes through, matched against the constants each module of the edited RTL multiplies by; two modules that share a coefficient set are both named, and the hover text gives the element and its constants. Verification comes from verification.yaml in the run dir when one was written; the loop itself does not run the equivalence checks. Its label is proven when the LEC closed, simulation under assumption when the simulation passed without a closed proof and the plan stated a bound it rests on, simulation only when it stated none; the hover text gives the simulation and LEC results and the stated bounds.

assumptions
`activation_type` does not change while the activation datapath is active, i.e. while `in_data_available | activation_in_progress` is high (line 5477). It is a cfg register written only over APB (line 4913) with reset value 1 (line 4838); the software configures it before writing start_tpu (line 4885) and the control FSM only raises the activation phase after start (lines 5259-5323). Under this assumption the hold cases of lines 5482-5485 (active, `activation_type==0`) only ever hold the value 0, because every edge on which the block is not active writes 0 to those registers (lines 5467-5471 and 5522-5526); driving the hard block's synchronous reset instead therefore produces the same value.
`inv_var` and `validity_mask` (= `validity_mask_a_rows`, line 5902) hold the same value on two consecutive clock edges while the norm datapath is active (line 5059). They are cfg registers written only over APB (lines 4889 and 4894, reset values at lines 4823 and 4829), and the software writes them before start_tpu (line 4885); the norm phase is entered only after start (lines 5259-5306). The rewrite samples them one edge earlier than lines 5068-5070 do, so it matches whenever they are steady across the pair of edges.

T2
plan header
BLOCKS 32 · TILES · STAGE_NS
assumptions
`activation_type` does not change while the activation datapath is active, i.e. while `in_data_available | activation_in_progress` is high (line 5477). It is a cfg register written only over APB (line 4913) with reset value 1 (line 4838); software configures it before writing start_tpu (line 4885) and the control FSM only raises the activation phase after start (lines 5259-5323). Under this assumption the hold cases of lines 5482-5485 (active, `activation_type==0`) only ever hold 0, because every edge on which the block is not active writes 0 to those registers (lines 5467-5471 and 5522-5526); and when `activation_type` is steadily 0 the whole tanh datapath is invisible anyway, since line 5534 then selects `relu_applied_data_internal`. Driving the hard block's synchronous reset with `~act_g` therefore produces the same visible value.
`inv_var` and `validity_mask` (= `validity_mask_a_rows`, line 5902) hold the same value on two consecutive clock edges while the norm datapath is active (line 5059). They are cfg registers written only over APB (lines 4889 and 4894, reset values at lines 4823 and 4829), written before start_tpu (line 4885); the norm phase is entered only after start (lines 5259-5306). The rewrite samples them one edge earlier than lines 5068-5070 do, so it matches whenever they are steady across the pair of edges.
